=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a tree rooted at node 0, consisting of n nodes numbered from 0 to n - 1. The tree is represented by an array parent of size n, where parent[i] is the parent of node i. Since node 0 is the root, parent[0] == -1.
You are also given a string s of length n, where s[i] is the character assigned to node i.
Consider an empty string dfsStr, and define a recursive function dfs(int x) that takes a node x as a parameter and performs the following steps in order:

Iterate over each child y of x in increasing order of their numbers, and call dfs(y).
Add the character s[x] to the end of the string dfsStr.

Note that dfsStr is shared across all recursive calls of dfs.
You need to find a boolean array answer of size n, where for each index i from 0 to n - 1, you do the following:

Empty the string dfsStr and call dfs(i).
If the resulting string dfsStr is a palindrome, then set answer[i] to true. Otherwise, set answer[i] to false.

Return the array answer.
 
Example 1:


Input: parent = [-1,0,0,1,1,2], s = "aababa"
Output: [true,true,false,true,true,true]
Explanation:

Calling dfs(0) results in the string dfsStr = "abaaba", which is a palindrome.
Calling dfs(1) results in the string dfsStr = "aba", which is a palindrome.
Calling dfs(2) results in the string dfsStr = "ab", which is not a palindrome.
Calling dfs(3) results in the string dfsStr = "a", which is a palindrome.
Calling dfs(4) results in the string dfsStr = "b", which is a palindrome.
Calling dfs(5) results in the string dfsStr = "a", which is a palindrome.


Example 2:


Input: parent = [-1,0,0,0,0], s = "aabcb"
Output: [true,true,true,true,true]
Explanation:
Every call on dfs(x) results in a palindrome string.

 
Constraints:

n == parent.length == s.length
1 <= n <= 10^5
0 <= parent[i] <= n - 1 for all i >= 1.
parent[0] == -1
parent represents a valid tree.
s consists only of lowercase English letters.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def findAnswer(self, parent: List[int], s: str) -> List[bool]:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Preorder order: parent, then descendants of child1, then child2... Reversed: descendants of child2, child2, descendants of child1, child1, parent. If we append s[x] in reversed preorder, we'd append child2's subtree before child1's subtree, not correct. Need postorder sorted children. We need actual DFS order.

Option 2: Use iterative stack frames to produce T in correct order and record starts. We can also compute start/length.

Let's design iterative DFS with stack frames.

children lists sorted ascending. We need process child in increasing order. Use stack = [(0, 0, False?)]? Let's define frame (x, idx, entered). When first visiting x:
- start[x] = len(T)
- if no children: append s[x]; length = 1; continue.
- else push frame to resume after child? Need process children sequentially.

Given string T length n.
d1[i] = number of odd palindromes centered at i, radius count including center. So substring [l,r] inclusive is palindrome if length odd and radius at center c=(l+r)//2 >= (r-l)//2 + 1. Equivalently d1[c] >= half+1.

Need implement Manacher O(n). Standard:
def manacher(s):
    n = len(s)
    d1 = [0]*n
    l=0; r=-1
    for i in range(n):
        k = 1 if i>r else min(d1[l+r-i], r-i+1)
        while i-k >=0 and i+k < n and s[i-k]==s[i+k]:
            k +=1
        d1[i]=k
        if i+k-1 > r:
            l = i-k+1; r = i+k-1
    d2 = [0]*n
    l=0; r=-1
    for i in range(n):
        k = 0 if i>r else min(d2[l+r-i+1], r-i+1)
        while i-k-1 >=0 and i+k < n and s[i-k-1]==s[i+k]:
            k +=1
        d2[i]=k
        if i+k-1 > r:
            l = i-k; r = i+k-1
    return d1,d2
Need check d2 mirror formula. Standard cp-algorithms:
vector<int> d2(n);
for (int i = 0, l = 0, r = -1; i < n; i++) {
    int k = (i > r) ? 0 : min(d2[l + r - i + 1], r - i + 1);
    while (i + k < n && i - k - 1 >= 0 && s[i + k] == s[i - k - 1]) {
        k++;
    }
    d2[i] = k;
    if (i + k - 1 > r) {
        l = i - k;
        r = i + k - 1;
    }
}
Yes.

Manacher code:

def manacher(t):
    n = len(t)
    d1 = [0]*n
    l = 0
    r = -1
    for i in range(n):
        if i > r:
            k = 1
        else:
            mirror = l + r - i
            k = min(d1[mirror], r - i + 1)
        while i - k >= 0 and i + k < n and t[i-k] == t[i+k]:
            k += 1
        d1[i] = k
        if i + k - 1 > r:
            l = i - k + 1
            r = i + k - 1

    d2 = [0]*n
    l = 0
    r = -1
    for i in range(n):
        if i > r:
            k = 0
        else:
            mirror = l + r - i + 1
            k = min(d2[mirror], r - i + 1)
        while i - k - 1 >= 0 and i + k < n and t[i-k-1] == t[i+k]:
            k += 1
        d2[i] = k
        if i + k - 1 > r:
            l = i - k
            r = i + k - 1
    return d1,d2

Need query function:
def is_pal(l, r): # half-open
    length = r - l
    if length <= 1: return True
    if length % 2 == 1:
        center = l + length // 2
        return d1[center] >= length // 2 + 1
    else:
        center = l + length // 2
        return d2[center] >= length // 2

Let's write final code:

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def findAnswer(self, parent: List[int], s: str) -> List[bool]:
        n = len(parent)
        children = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        for i, p in enumerate(parent):
            if p != -1:
                children[p].append(i)

        start = [0] * n
        length = [0] * n
        dfs_str = []

        stack = [(0, False)]
        while stack:
            x, done = stack.pop()
            if not done:
                start[x] = len(dfs_str)
                stack.append((x, True))
                # push children in reverse so smallest is processed first
                for y in reversed(children[x]):
                    stack.append((y, False))
            else:
                dfs_str.append(s[x])
                length[x] = len(dfs_str) - start[x]

        t = ''.join(dfs_str)

        # Manacher
        d1 = [0] * n
        l = 0
        r = -1
        for i in range(n):
            if i > r:
                k = 1
            else:
                k = min(d1[l + r - i], r - i + 1)
            while i - k >= 0 and i + k < n and t[i - k] == t[i + k]:
                k += 1
            d1[i] = k
            if i + k - 1 > r:
                l = i - k + 1
                r = i + k - 1

        d2 = [0] * n
        l = 0
        r = -1
        for i in range(n):
            if i > r:
                k = 0
            else:
                k = min(d2[l + r - i + 1], r - i + 1)
            while i - k - 1 >= 0 and i + k < n and t[i - k - 1] == t[i + k]:
                k += 1
            d2[i] = k
            if i + k - 1 > r:
                l = i - k
                r = i + k - 1

        ans = [False] * n
        for i in range(n):
            L = length[i]
            if L == 1:
                ans[i] = True
            elif L & 1:
                center = start[i] + L // 2
                ans[i] = d1[center] >= L // 2 + 1
            else:
                center = start[i] + L // 2
                ans[i] = d2[center] >= L // 2
        return ans

</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def findAnswer(self, parent: List[int], s: str) -> List[bool]:
        n = len(parent)

        children = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        for i, p in enumerate(parent):
            if p != -1:
                children[p].append(i)

        start = [0] * n
        length = [0] * n
        dfs_str = []

        # Iterative DFS producing exactly dfs(0)'s string.
        stack = [(0, False)]
        while stack:
            x, finalized = stack.pop()
            if not finalized:
                start[x] = len(dfs_str)
                stack.append((x, True))
                # Push children in reverse so the smallest is processed first.
                for y in reversed(children[x]):
                    stack.append((y, False))
            else:
                dfs_str.append(s[x])
                length[x] = len(dfs_str) - start[x]

        t = ''.join(dfs_str)

        # Manacher: odd-length palindromes
        d1 = [0] * n
        l, r = 0, -1
        for i in range(n):
            if i > r:
                k = 1
            else:
                k = min(d1[l + r - i], r - i + 1)

            while i - k >= 0 and i + k < n and t[i - k] == t[i + k]:
                k += 1

            d1[i] = k
            if i + k - 1 > r:
                l = i - k + 1
                r = i + k - 1

        # Manacher: even-length palindromes
        d2 = [0] * n
        l, r = 0, -1
        for i in range(n):
            if i > r:
                k = 0
            else:
                k = min(d2[l + r - i + 1], r - i + 1)

            while i - k - 1 >= 0 and i + k < n and t[i - k - 1] == t[i + k]:
                k += 1

            d2[i] = k
            if i + k - 1 > r:
                l = i - k
                r = i + k - 1

        ans = [False] * n
        for i in range(n):
            L = length[i]
            if L == 1:
                ans[i] = True
            elif L & 1:
                center = start[i] + L // 2
                ans[i] = d1[center] >= L // 2 + 1
            else:
                center = start[i] + L // 2
                ans[i] = d2[center] >= L // 2

        return ans
```
